(Incremental stabilizability)
.

There exist a feedback law
{{\kappa^{\delta}}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}:{{{\mathcal{X}}^{2}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}^{{n^{\mathrm{u}}}}}}
, an incremental Lyapunov function
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}:{{{\mathcal{X}}^{2}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}}
that is continuously differentiable and satisfies
{V^{\delta}}({{\bm{z}},{\bm{z}}})=0,\forall{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{X}}
, parameters
{c^{\delta\mathrm{,l}}},{c^{\delta\mathrm{,u}}},\rho>0
, and Lipschitz constants
{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}>0,j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}}
and
{c^{\mathrm{o}}}>0
, such that the following properties hold for all
({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})\in{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}}
,
{\bm{w}}\in{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}\oplus{\mathcal{W}}
, and
{\bm{\eta}}\in H
:

\displaystyle{c^{\delta\mathrm{,l}}}\lVert{\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{z}}\rVert^{2}\leq{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}\leq{c^{\delta\mathrm{,u}}}\lVert{\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{z}}\rVert^{2},

(151a)

\displaystyle{g_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})})-{g_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}({{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})\leq{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}},\

\displaystyle j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}},

(151b)

\displaystyle{g^{\mathrm{o}}}(M{\hat{\bm{x}}})-{g^{\mathrm{o}}}(M{\bm{z}})\leq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}},

(151c)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}\leq-2\rho{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})},

(151d)

with
{\dot{\hat{\bm{x}}}}=f({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})})+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+L({\bm{y}}-C{\hat{\bm{x}}})
,
{\dot{\bm{z}}}=f({{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}
,
{\bm{y}}
defined in (
146
), and
{\dot{\bm{x}}}=f({\bm{x}},{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})})+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}
. Furthermore, the following norm-like inequality holds
\forall{\bm{x}}_{1},{\bm{x}}_{2},{\bm{x}}_{3}\in{\mathbb{R}^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}
:

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{x}}_{1},{\bm{x}}_{3})}\leq\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{x}}_{1},{\bm{x}}_{2})}+\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{x}}_{2},{\bm{x}}_{3})}.

(152)

This assumption reflects the same properties as Assumption
3
in Section
4.3
with the only difference that it is defined with
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
instead of
{\bm{x}}
using observer (
150
). Following similar arguments as in Section
4.3
, we know that the time-derivative of
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
can be upper-bounded:

Property 3
.

There exists a constant
{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}>0
such that for any
({\bm{y}},{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}})\in{\mathbb{R}^{{n^{\mathrm{y}}}}}\times{\mathbb{R}^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}
, any
{\bm{w}}\in{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}\oplus{\mathcal{W}}
, and any
{\bm{\eta}}\in H
, it holds that

\frac{d}{dt}\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}\leq-\rho\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}+{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},

(153)

with
{\dot{\hat{\bm{x}}}}=f({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})})+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+L({\bm{y}}-C{\hat{\bm{x}}})
,
{\dot{\bm{z}}}=f({{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}
,
{\bm{y}}
defined in (
146
), and
{\dot{\bm{x}}}=f({\bm{x}},{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})})+E{\bm{w}}
.

Note that a different constant
{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}\neq{\bar{w}}
is used in this case. Whereas
{\bar{w}}
accounts for the impact of disturbances,
{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}
accounts for the impact of both disturbances and measurement noise.

By Property
3
, we know that
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
is upper-bounded by tube size
s
:

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}\leq s,

(154)

with the tube dynamics given by

{\dot{s}}=-\rho s+{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},

(155)

which is the same as (
148e
). Rewriting as a function of time gives

s_{t}=\left(1-e^{-\rho t}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho},

(156)

and taking the limit
t\rightarrow\infty
results in a maximum tube size of

{\bar{s}}=\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}.

(157)

Thus,
s
does not grow unbounded, and the resulting tube size (
157
) is suitable for tightening the constraints.

Note that contraction property (
151d
) and corresponding Property
3
and tube dynamics (
148e
) are used to upper-bound the controller error

{\bm{\delta}}\coloneqq{\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{z}}.

(158)

Thus, tightening using
s
results in closed-loop safety guarantees for
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
, not for
{\bm{x}}
.

To account for the observer error

{\bm{\epsilon}}\coloneqq{\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}},

(159)

we can make the following assumption:

Assumption 8

(Bounded observer error)
.

There observer gain matrix
L
is such that the observer error
{\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}
is bounded and described by an
\epsilon
-sublevel set of
{\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}}}
:

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{x}}_{t},{\hat{\bm{x}}}_{t})}\leq\epsilon,\ \forall t\geq 0.

(160)

This assumption implies that we can additionally tighten the system and obstacle avoidance constraints proportional to
\epsilon
in order to guarantee closed-loop safety of both
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
and
{\bm{x}}
. Note that the tightening proportional to
\epsilon
only applies to the state constraints, not the input constraints, since control law (
149
) is independent of
{\bm{x}}
. Therefore, we introduce additional tightening constants
{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s,o}}},\ j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}}
:

{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s,o}}}=\begin{cases}0,&j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{[1,2{n^{\mathrm{u}}}]}\\
{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}},&j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{[2{n^{\mathrm{u}}}+1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}\end{cases}.

(161)

Similar to Section
4.3
, we need to assume the existence of the following terminal ingredients:

Assumption 9

(Terminal ingredients)
.

There exist a terminal control law
{\kappa^{\mathrm{f}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{r}})}:{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\bar{\mathcal{Z}}}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}^{{n^{\mathrm{u}}}}}
and terminal cost
{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}},{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}}):{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\bar{{\mathcal{X}}}}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
such that the following property holds for all
{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{X}}
:

{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}}_{{{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+T}|t}}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+T}}^{\mathrm{r}})-{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}}_{{T|t}}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T}}^{\mathrm{r}})\leq-\int_{T}^{T+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{s}}}({\bm{z}}_{{\tau|t}}^{*},{\bm{v}}_{{\tau|t}}^{*},{\bm{r}}_{{t+\tau}})d\tau,

(162)

with sampling time
{T^{\mathrm{s}}}>0
, optimal solution
({\bm{z}}_{{\cdot|t}}^{*},{\bm{v}}_{{\cdot|t}}^{*})
at
t
for all
t\geq 0
and its extended version
{\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*}
using terminal control law
{\kappa^{\mathrm{f}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{r}})}
for
\tau\in[0,{T^{\mathrm{s}}}]
, as defined in Section
5.3.1
.

Similar to Section
4.3.4
, next to this assumption on terminal control law
{\kappa^{\mathrm{f}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{r}})}
and terminal cost
{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}},{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})
, we want to design terminal set
{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})
such that tightened system and obstacle avoidance constraints (
148f
) and (
148g
) are satisfied in
{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})
, respectively, and such that terminal control law
{\kappa^{\mathrm{f}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{r}})}
ensures robust positive invariance of
{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})
. The specific formulation of a tight version of
{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})
is presented in Section
5.3.2
as part of the proof and therefore not included in Assumption
9
.

Figure 3:
Visualization of a 1D reference trajectory
{\bm{x}}_{t+[0,4{T^{\mathrm{s}}}]}^{\mathrm{r}}
, actual state trajectory
{\bm{x}}_{[t,{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}]}
, previously optimal solution
{\bm{z}}_{[0,T]|t}^{*}
starting from estimated state
{\hat{\bm{x}}}_{t}
, appended part of previously optimal solution
{\bm{z}}_{[T,T+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}]|t}^{*}
, candidate solution
{\tilde{\bm{z}}}_{[0,T]|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}
starting from estimated state
{\hat{\bm{x}}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}
, tubes
{V^{\delta}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}^{*})\leq s
and
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}\leq\epsilon
around
{\bm{z}}_{[0,T]|t}^{*}
, tubes
{V^{\delta}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\tilde{\bm{z}}})\leq s
and
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}\leq\epsilon
around
{\tilde{\bm{z}}}_{[0,T]|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}
, and terminal sets
{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})
around the reference trajectory corresponding to the previously optimal and the candidate solution. Again, we set
T=4{T^{\mathrm{s}}}
. Note that the Minkowski sum of tubes
s
and
\epsilon
is plotted around the nominal predicted trajectory as the combination of these tubes is used to tighten the constraints. Compared to Figure
2
, the initial tube size is centered around
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
, has a non-zero size, and contains state
{\bm{x}}
. Again,
{\bm{w}}
impacts the system during interval \raisebox{-0.9pt}{1}⃝. Thus, the differences
{\tilde{\bm{z}}}_{{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}|t}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}
and
{\bm{x}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}
are both the result of the combination of
{\bm{w}}
,
{\bm{\eta}}
, and observer dynamics (
150
). Note that the tubes and terminal sets are visualized as ellipses to clarify their shapes, whereas they should be visualized as vertical lines in this case.

Again, the assumptions above allow us to prove recursive feasibility and trajectory tracking. Figure
3
provides intuition for the proofs related to ROMPC formulation (
148
). Compared to the RMPC case, instead of
{\bm{x}}
, we can only obtain estimated state
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
via noisy output measurements
{\bm{y}}
. However, by suitably designing observer (
150
), we know that
{\bm{x}}
is contained in a tube around
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
. Therefore, the system and obstacle avoidance constraints are tightened by an additional term propertional to this tube size. Furthermore, the tube size used to describe the difference between
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
and
{\bm{z}}
starts from zero at the beginning of the prediction horizon and is strictly larger than the tube size designed in Section
4.3
for both the previously optimal and the candidate solution. Given a suitably designed control law and incremental Lyapunov function, the candidate contracts with at least factor
\rho
towards the previously optimal solution. Hence, with the adjusted tube sizes above, we can apply a similar reasoning as in Section
4.3.2
to prove recursive feasibility.

Since the feedback law is based on
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
rather than
{\bm{x}}
, the candidate might contract slower to the previously optimal solution compared to the case where
{\bm{x}}
is used to compute the feedback law. This worst-case effect results in a larger tracking error between
{\bm{x}}
and the reference trajectory. Since we know that
{\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}
is bounded, we can still conclude that, on average, the tracking error gets small. However, the bound is less strict than the one obtained in Section
4.3.3
.

5.3.1
Candidate solution

Similar to Section
4.3.1
, the goal of defining the candidate solution is to show that there exists a feasible, not necessarily optimal, solution to (
148
) at time
{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}
given that (
148
) is successfully solved at time
t
. Again, this candidate solution is used to prove trajectory tracking and recursive feasibility, but it is not necessarily implemented in practice.

In the robust output-feedback case, we pick a candidate solution similar to the nominal case. For convenience, we define for
\tau\in[T,T+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}]
:

{\bm{v}}_{{\tau|t}}^{*}={\kappa^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}}_{{\tau|t}}^{*},{\bm{r}}_{{t+\tau}}),

(163)

with
{\dot{\bm{z}}}_{{\tau|t}}^{*}
given by (
1
) by applying (
163
).

Given these definitions, we can write the candidate for prediction interval
\tau\in[0,T]
at time
{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}
as

\displaystyle{\tilde{\bm{z}}}_{0|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}

\displaystyle={\hat{\bm{x}}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}},

(164a)

\displaystyle{\tilde{s}}_{0}

\displaystyle=0,

(164b)

\displaystyle{\tilde{\bm{v}}}_{{\tau|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}}

\displaystyle=\kappa({\tilde{\bm{z}}}_{{\tau|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}},{\bm{z}}_{{{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}|t}}^{*},{\bm{v}}_{{{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}|t}}^{*}),

(164c)

with
{\dot{\tilde{\bm{z}}}}_{{\tau|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}}
and
{\dot{\tilde{s}}}_{\tau}
according to (
148d
) and (
148e
), respectively.

Note that this candidate deviates from the one in Section
4.3.1
. The main difference is the initial estimated state. Consequently, feedback control law (
164c
) gives different values compared to (
56c
) in Section
4.3.1
.

5.3.2
Recursive feasibility proof

The recursive feasibility proof follows similar arguments as in Section
4.3.2
with additional technicalities to account for measurement noise
{\bm{\eta}}
. Instead of optimizing from the measured state
{\bm{x}}
, the optimization starts from the estimated state
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
, and we ensure that the controller error
{\bm{\delta}}
is contained inside
\delta
-sublevel set of
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
and the observer error
{\bm{\epsilon}}
inside
\epsilon
-sublevel set of
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
.

To prove recursive feasibility, first note that the initial state, initial tube, system dynamics, and tube dynamics constraints are all trivially satisfied by (
164
). Again, we have to show that the system constraints (
148f
), the obstacle avoidance constraints (
148g
), and the terminal set constraint (
148h
) are satisfied for
\tau\in[0,T]
.

Similar to the procedure in Section
4.3.2
, we split the interval into two subintervals to show satisfaction of (
148f
) and (
148g
):
\tau\in[0,T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}})
and
\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]
. By replacing
{\bar{w}}
by
{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}
and additionally tightening the state constraints with a factor proportional to
\epsilon
, we obtain trivial satisfaction of the system and obstacle avoidance constraints for
\tau\in[0,T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}})
following the same procedure as in Section
4.3.2
. Note that in that case the additional tightening terms proportional to
\epsilon
cancel out in (
62
) and the same proof in Appendix
B
holds with a different expression for
s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}
:

s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}=\left(1-e^{-\rho{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}.

(165)

Terminal set constraint satisfaction for
\bm{\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]}

Since the tightening is increased proportional to
\epsilon
compared to Section
4
, we need to account for this additional tube size in the terminal set. Therefore, the terminal set is now given by

{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})\coloneqq\left\{{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{X}},s_{T}\in{\mathbb{R}},\epsilon\in{\mathbb{R}}\ \middle|\ \sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}},{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})}+s_{T}+\epsilon\leq\alpha\right\}.

(166)

To show satisfaction of terminal set constraint (
148h
) for
\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]
, we follow similar steps to the ones outlined in Remark
10
in Section
4.3.2
. We start by showing that terminal set constraint (
166
) is satisfied at
\tau=T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}}
at
t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}
. This gives an inequality similar to (
82a
) evaluated at
\tau=0
:

\alpha-s_{T}-\epsilon+e^{-\rho(T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}})}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}\leq\alpha-s_{T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}-\epsilon,

(167)

or, equivalently,

s_{T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}\leq s_{T}-e^{-\rho(T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}})}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}.

(168)

This is the same expression as (
68
), which holds trivially by the proof in Appendix
B
with
s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}
given by (
165
).

Appendix
F
proves that terminal set (
166
) is invariant provided that
\alpha\geq\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+\epsilon
. Therefore, we can conclude that the terminal set constraint is also satisfied with this lower bound on
\alpha
for
\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]
at
{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}
.

System constraints satisfaction for
\bm{\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]}

Following the same reasoning as detailed in Section
4.3.2
and leveraging the proof in Appendix
B
and the fact that (
76
) is invariant, the candidate system constraints are satisfied for
\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]
under the following assumption:

Assumption 10

(System constraints tightening for reference trajectory)
.

The reference trajectory satisfies the tightened systems constraints, i.e., (
7
) holds, with
{g_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{u}})
given by

{g_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{u}})={g_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{u}})+{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s,o}}}\epsilon,\ j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}},

(169)

where
{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}
and
{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s,o}}}
are defined in (
151b
) and (
161
), respectively.

This is the same assumption as Assumption
5
in Section
4.3.2
with the only difference that we have access to
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
instead of
{\bm{x}}
and, correspondingly, the lower bound on
\alpha
is larger.

Obstacle avoidance constraints satisfaction for
\bm{\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]}

Following a similar reasoning as for the system constraints satisfaction for
\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]
, the candidate obstacle avoidance constraints are satisfied for
\tau\in[T-{T^{\mathrm{s}}},T]
under the following assumption:

Assumption 11

(Obstacle avoidance constraints tightening for reference trajectory)
.

The reference trajectory satisfies the tightened obstacle avoidance constraints, i.e., (
9
) holds, with
{g_{j}^{\mathrm{r,o}}}({\hat{\bm{p}}})
given by

{g_{j}^{\mathrm{r,o}}}({\hat{\bm{p}}})={g_{j}^{\mathrm{o}}}({\hat{\bm{p}}})+{c^{\mathrm{o}}}\alpha,\ j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{o}}}]}},

(170)

where
{c^{\mathrm{o}}}
is defined in (
151c
) and
\alpha\geq\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+\epsilon
.

Again, this assumption is the same as Assumption
6
in Section
4.3.2
with the only difference that we have availability of
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
instead of
{\bm{x}}
and, correspondingly, the lower bound on
\alpha
is larger.
∎

5.3.3
Trajectory tracking proof

The trajectory tracking proof follows the procedure detailed in Section
4.3.3
. The only difference is that we show tracking for a trajectory optimized from
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
instead of
{\bm{x}}
. Correspondingly, we can follow the same proof as outlined in Section
4.3.3
with the exceptions that we now leverage (
162
) in Assumption
9
instead of (
54
) in Assumption
4
. The upper bound on the difference in stage costs is now given by

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{s}}}({\tilde{\bm{z}}}_{{\tau|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}},{\tilde{\bm{v}}}_{{\tau|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}},{\bm{r}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}})-{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{s}}}({\bm{z}}_{{{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}|t}}^{*},{\bm{v}}_{{{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}|t}}^{*},{\bm{r}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}})

\displaystyle\leq{L^{\mathrm{{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{s}}},xu}}}e^{-\rho\tau}\left(1-e^{\rho{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}\eqqcolon\alpha_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}}),

(171)

instead of (
4.3.3
), and that the upper bound on the difference in terminal costs is given by

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\tilde{\bm{z}}}_{{T|{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}},{\bm{x}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+T}}^{\mathrm{r}})-{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}}_{{{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+T}|t}}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+T}}^{\mathrm{r}})

\displaystyle\leq{L^{\mathrm{{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{f}}}}}}e^{-\rho T}\left(1-e^{\rho{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}\eqqcolon\alpha^{\mathrm{f}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}}),

(172)

instead of (
4.3.3
). Following similar steps as in Section
4.3.3
with different expressions for
\alpha_{\tau}^{\mathrm{s}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}})
,
\alpha^{\mathrm{f}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}})
, and
{\bar{\alpha}^{\mathrm{s,f}}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}})
changes (
109
) into the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\lVert{\bm{x}}_{\tau}-{\bm{x}}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert_{Q}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}c^{\mathrm{t,1}}\lVert{\bm{x}}_{\tau}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}_{\tau}\rVert+c^{\mathrm{t,2}}\lVert{\hat{\bm{x}}}_{\tau}-{\bm{z}}_{\tau-t|t}^{*}\rVert+\lVert{\bm{z}}_{\tau-t|t}^{*}-{\bm{x}}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert_{Q}^{2}

(173a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{\leq}c^{\mathrm{t,3}}\epsilon+c^{\mathrm{t,4}}s_{\tau}+\lVert{\bm{z}}_{\tau-t|t}^{*}-{\bm{x}}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert_{Q}^{2},

(173b)

where (a) is obtained by Lipschitz bounds with constants
c^{\mathrm{t,1}}
and
c^{\mathrm{t,2}}
and (b) by other Lipschitz bounds with constants
c^{\mathrm{t,3}}
and
c^{\mathrm{t,4}}
on the norms using a combination of (
44a
), (
160
), (
154
), and (
156
). Therefore, we can write a similar upper bound as in (
110
):

\displaystyle c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}\int_{t}^{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}\lVert{\bm{x}}_{\tau}-{\bm{x}}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert_{Q}^{2}d\tau\leq

\displaystyle c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}\int_{t}^{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}\lVert{\bm{z}}_{\tau-t|t}^{*}-{\bm{x}}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert_{Q}^{2}+c^{\mathrm{t,4}}s_{\tau}+c^{\mathrm{t,3}}\epsilon d\tau

(174a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{=}

\displaystyle c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}\int_{t}^{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}\lVert{\bm{z}}_{\tau-t|t}^{*}-{\bm{x}}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert_{Q}^{2}d\tau+c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}\int_{t}^{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}c^{\mathrm{t,4}}s_{\tau}d\tau

\displaystyle+c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}\int_{t}^{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}c^{\mathrm{t,3}}\epsilon d\tau

(174b)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{t}^{*}-{\mathcal{J}}_{{t+{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}}^{*}+{\bar{\alpha}^{\mathrm{s,f,1}}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}}),

(174c)

with
{\bar{\alpha}^{\mathrm{s,f,1}}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}})\coloneqq{\bar{\alpha}^{\mathrm{s,f}}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}})+c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}c^{\mathrm{t,4}}\left({T^{\mathrm{s}}}-\frac{1}{\rho}\left(1-e^{-\rho{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}\right)\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}{T^{\mathrm{s}}}c^{\mathrm{t,3}}\epsilon
, and where (a) is obtained by writing out the integral expressions and (b) by (
108
). Following the same steps as in Section
4.3.3
, we obtain upper bound

\displaystyle\underset{t\rightarrow\infty}{{\operatorname{lim}}}\frac{\int_{0}^{t}\lVert{\bm{x}}_{\tau}-{\bm{x}}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert_{Q}^{2}d\tau}{t}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{J}}_{0}^{*}-\underset{t\rightarrow\infty}{{\operatorname{lim}}}{\mathcal{J}}_{t}^{*}}{c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}t}+\frac{1}{c^{{\mathcal{J}},\mathrm{d}}{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}{\bar{\alpha}^{\mathrm{s,f,1}}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}}),

(175)

By applying Barbalat’s lemma
[
29
]
on
\frac{\int_{0}^{2{T^{\mathrm{s}}}}\lVert{\bm{x}}_{\tau}-{\bm{x}}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert_{Q}^{2}d\tau}{t}
we conclude that, on average, the tracking error
\lVert{\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\mathrm{r}}\rVert
gets small over time, in this case proportional to
{\bar{\alpha}^{\mathrm{s,f,1}}}(\rho,{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},{T^{\mathrm{s}}})
.
∎

5.3.4
Design to satisfy the assumptions

This section will detail the design of:

•

the obstacle avoidance constraints, which need to satisfy Assumption
2
;

•

incremental Lyapunov function
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
and corresponding Lipschitz constants
{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}},{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s,o}}},j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}}
and
{c^{\mathrm{o}}}
, which need to satisfy Assumptions
7
and
8
;

•

the terminal ingredients, which need to satisfy Assumption
9
;

•

the reference trajectory, which needs to satisfy Assumptions
10
and
11
.

Obstacle avoidance constraints design

To prove recursive feasibility, we need the obstacle avoidance constraint to satisfy Assumption
2
. We can achieve this by following the same design as written in Section
3.2.4
.

Incremental stabilizability design

Following similar arguments as in Section
4.3.4
, the goal of the incremental stabilizability design is to find constant metric
{P^{\delta}}
and state-dependent feedback
{K^{\delta}({{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{x}}(s)})}
such that we can construct an upper bound on the incremental Lyapunov function. Since the tube dynamics in this case are defined for
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
instead of
{\bm{x}}
, we would like to find the upper bound on
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
instead of
{V^{\delta}({{\bm{x}},{\bm{z}}})}
in the following form:

\frac{d}{dt}{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}\leq 2{\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}}\left(-\rho{\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}}+{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}\right)=-2\rho{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}+2{\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}}{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}},

(176)

with incremental Lyapunov function
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
defined as

{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}\coloneqq({\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{z}})^{\top}{P^{\delta}}({\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{z}}),

(177)

geodesic
{{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{x}}(s)}
defined in (
130
), and control law
{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})}
defined as

{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})}={{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{u}}}(1)={\bm{v}}+\int_{0}^{1}{K^{\delta}({{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{x}}(s)})}({\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{z}})ds,

(178)

such that (
148e
) is a valid equation for the tube dynamics. To this end, let’s first define the closed-loop observer dynamics:

\displaystyle{\dot{\hat{\bm{x}}}}

\displaystyle=f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+L({\bm{y}}-C{\hat{\bm{x}}})

\displaystyle=f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+L({\bm{y}}-C({\bm{x}}+{\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))

\displaystyle=f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}})),

(179)

with
{\bm{\eta}}
defined in (
146
).

Using the following shorthand notations and leveraging (
5.3.4
):

\displaystyle{\dot{{\bm{\delta}}}}\coloneqq

\displaystyle{\dot{\hat{\bm{x}}}}-{\dot{\bm{z}}}

(180)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))-\left(f({\bm{z}},{\bm{v}})-E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))-f({\bm{z}},{\bm{v}})+L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}})),

(181)

\displaystyle{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}\coloneqq

\displaystyle{A({{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{x}}},{{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{u}}})}+{B({{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{x}}},{{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{u}}})}{K^{\delta}({{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{x}}})},

(182)

we can upper-bound the time-derivative of
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}=

\displaystyle{\dot{{\bm{\delta}}}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}+{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\dot{{\bm{\delta}}}}

(183a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\dot{{\bm{\delta}}}}

(183b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))-f({\bm{z}},{\bm{v}})+L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right)

(183c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))-f({\bm{z}},{\bm{v}})\right)+2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))

(183d)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}{\bm{\delta}}+2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))

(183e)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle-2\rho{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}+2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))

(183f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\rho{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}+2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))

(183g)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2\rho{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}+2\left\lVert{\bm{\delta}}\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}\left\lVert L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}

(183h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\rho{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}+2\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}\left\lVert L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}

(183i)

where (a) is obtained by knowing that there exists an
s\in[0,1]
such that this expression holds by the MVT and (b) by contraction LMI (
139c
). Applying the
{\operatorname{sqrt}}
operator to the last expression yields

\frac{d}{dt}{\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}}\leq-\rho{\sqrt{{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}}}+\left\lVert L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}.

(184)

Comparing this equation to (
148e
) indicates that we need to upper-bound
\left\lVert L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}
for all
({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{x}},{\bm{\eta}})\in{{\mathcal{X}}^{2}}\times H
to obtain
{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}
should upper-bound
\left\lVert L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}
. We design
L
with bounded eigenvalues and we know that
{\bm{\eta}}
is bounded since
{\bm{\eta}}\in H
with compact
H
. This leaves us to show that
{\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}
is bounded for all
({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})\in{{\mathcal{X}}^{2}}
.

A sufficient condition to show boundedness of
{\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}
is to enforce that sublevel set of
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
(
160
) is RPI. Similar to RPI LMI (
139d
) this gives the following LMI:

\begin{bmatrix}{X^{\delta}}\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}-LC\right)^{\top}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}-LC\right){X^{\delta}}+{\lambda^{\epsilon}}{X^{\delta}}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}&-{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}\end{bmatrix}\preceq 0,

(185)

with multiplier
{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\geq 0
and for
{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\operatorname{vert}}({\mathcal{W}})
and
{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H)
. The proof of this so-called
\epsilon
-RPI LMI is provided in Appendix
G
.

Remark 18
.

Since LMI (
185
) enforces (
160
) to be RPI, the presented results below are only valid if (
160
) is satisfied at
t=0
, i.e.,
\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{x}}_{0},{\hat{\bm{x}}}_{0})}\leq\epsilon
.

Given metric
{P^{\delta}}
satisfying this LMI, we can write the upper bound on
\left\lVert L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}
as

\displaystyle\left\lVert L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}L\left(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}})\right)\right\rVert

(186a)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LF{\bm{\eta}}+{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}})\right\rVert

(186b)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LF{\bm{\eta}}\right\rVert+\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}})\right\rVert

(186c)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LF{\bm{\eta}}\right\rVert+\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}})\right\rVert

(186d)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LF{\bm{\eta}}\right\rVert+\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}})\right\rVert

(186e)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert LF{\bm{\eta}}\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}+\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\left\lVert{\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}

(186f)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert LF{\bm{\eta}}\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}+\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\epsilon,

(186g)

with
{\bm{\eta}}\in H
. Since
H
is a convex polytopic set, evaluating this expression at the vertices of
H
suffices. Thus,
{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}
is given by

{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}\coloneqq\underset{{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H)}{{\operatorname{max}}}\left\lVert LF{\bm{\eta}}\right\rVert_{{P^{\delta}}}+\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\epsilon.

(187)

LMI (
185
) can also be used to optimize the shape of the tube using an RPI LMI for the controller error similar to (
138
). Let’s call this LMI the
\delta
-RPI LMI. It is given by

\begin{bmatrix}{A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\right)^{\top}+{\lambda^{\delta}}{X^{\delta}}&LC{X^{\delta}}&LF{\bm{\eta}}\\
\left(LC{X^{\delta}}\right)^{\top}&-{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}{X^{\delta}}&\bm{0}\\
\left(LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}&\bm{0}&{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}-{\lambda^{\delta}}\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}\preceq 0,

(188)

with
{\bm{\zeta}}\in{\mathcal{Z}}
,
{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H)
and multipliers
{\lambda^{\delta}},{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}\geq 0
. Similar to the design in Section
4.3.4
, we can set
\delta=1
without loss of generality. Note that
\epsilon
in LMI (
185
) does not necessarily equal 1. Instead, it should hold that
{\lambda^{\delta}}\delta^{2}\geq{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}
for (
188
) to be satisfied. Since this LMI has a linear dependency on
{\bm{\eta}}
and the set
H
is convex, it suffices to check (
188
) at the vertices of
H
. Appendix
H
provides the proof of (
188
).

Compared to the RMPC case, we want to solve the same SDP but with replacing RPI LMI (
138
) by the combination of
\epsilon
-RPI LMI (
185
) and
\delta
-RPI LMI (
188
) and weighting decision variables
{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}},j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{[2{n^{\mathrm{u}}}+1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}
and
{c^{\mathrm{o}}}
differently in the cost function according to the additional state constraints tightening proportional to
\epsilon
:

\displaystyle\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}{X^{\delta}},{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\\
{{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}}^{2},{{c^{\mathrm{o}}}}^{2}\end{subarray}}{{\operatorname{min}}}\ \

\displaystyle(1+\epsilon^{2}){c^{\mathrm{c,o}}}{{c^{\mathrm{o}}}}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{2{n^{\mathrm{u}}}}{c_{j}^{\mathrm{c,s}}}{{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}}^{2}+(1+\epsilon^{2})\sum_{j=2{n^{\mathrm{u}}}+1}^{{n^{\mathrm{s}}}}{c_{j}^{\mathrm{c,s}}}{{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}}^{2},

(189a)

\displaystyle\operatorname{s.t.}\

\displaystyle{X^{\delta}}\succeq 0,

(189b)

\displaystyle{A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\right)^{\top}+2\rho{X^{\delta}}\preceq 0,

(189c)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\right)^{\top}+{\lambda^{\delta}}{X^{\delta}}&LC{X^{\delta}}&LF{\bm{\eta}}\\
\left(LC{X^{\delta}}\right)^{\top}&-{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}{X^{\delta}}&\bm{0}\\
\left(LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}&\bm{0}&{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}-{\lambda^{\delta}}\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}\preceq 0,

(189d)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{X^{\delta}}\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}-LC\right)^{\top}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}-LC\right){X^{\delta}}+{\lambda^{\epsilon}}{X^{\delta}}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}&-{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}\end{bmatrix}\preceq 0,

(189e)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}}^{2}&{L_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}\begin{bmatrix}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\\
{X^{\delta}}\end{bmatrix}\\
\left({L_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}\begin{bmatrix}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\\
{X^{\delta}}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{\top}&{X^{\delta}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0,\ j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}},

(189f)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{{c^{\mathrm{o}}}}^{2}I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}&M{X^{\delta}}\\
\left(M{X^{\delta}}\right)^{\top}&{X^{\delta}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0,

(189g)

\displaystyle{\bm{\zeta}}\in{\mathcal{Z}},\quad{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\operatorname{vert}}\left({\mathcal{W}}\right),\quad{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H),

contraction rate
\rho>0
and multipliers
{\lambda^{\delta}},{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}},{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\geq 0
that can be computed using bi-section over this SDP, user-chosen observer gain matrix
L\in{\mathbb{R}^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}\times{n^{\mathrm{y}}}}}
, and sublevel sets
\delta,\epsilon>0
. Similar to the design in Section
4.3.4
, we set
\delta=1
and we need to make sure
{\lambda^{\delta}}\delta^{2}\geq{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}
holds. This SDP is semi-infinite, so we need to evaluate the expressions at grid points and vertices, in this case in
{\mathcal{Z}}
,
{\mathcal{W}}
, and
H
, to account for both the effect of disturbances and measurement noise.

Remark 19
.

To reduce computation time, we split LMI (
189d
) following the same procedure as outlined in Remark
16
:

\begin{bmatrix}0^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}&0^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}&LF{\bm{\eta}}\\
0^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}&0^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}&\bm{0}\\
\left(LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}&\bm{0}&0\end{bmatrix}\preceq{\bar{H}},\quad{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H),

(190)

{\bar{H}}+\begin{bmatrix}{A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\right)^{\top}+{\lambda^{\delta}}{X^{\delta}}&LC{X^{\delta}}&\bm{0}\\
\left(LC{X^{\delta}}\right)^{\top}&-{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}{X^{\delta}}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\bm{0}&{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}-{\lambda^{\delta}}\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}\preceq 0,\quad{\bm{\zeta}}\in{\mathcal{Z}}.

(191)

Similarly, we split LMI (
189e
) as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}0^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}\preceq{\bar{{\mathcal{W}}}},\quad

\displaystyle{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\operatorname{vert}}({\mathcal{W}}),

(192)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}0^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}&-LF{\bm{\eta}}\\
\left(-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}\preceq{\bar{H}_{1}},\quad

\displaystyle{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H),

(193)

{\bar{{\mathcal{W}}}}+{\bar{H}_{1}}+\begin{bmatrix}{X^{\delta}}\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}-LC\right)^{\top}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}-LC\right){X^{\delta}}+{\lambda^{\epsilon}}{X^{\delta}}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&-{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}\end{bmatrix}\preceq 0,\quad{\bm{\zeta}}\in{\mathcal{Z}}.

(194)

Remark 20
.

An alternative objective to (
189a
) is

\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}{X^{\delta}},{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\\
{{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}}^{2},{{c^{\mathrm{o}}}}^{2},,\epsilon^{2}\end{subarray}}{{\operatorname{min}}}\ \ {c^{\mathrm{c,o}}}{{c^{\mathrm{o}}}}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{{n^{\mathrm{s}}}}{c_{j}^{\mathrm{c,s}}}{{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}}^{2}+c^{\epsilon}\epsilon^{2},

(195)

in which
\epsilon^{2}
is an additional decision variable and
c^{\epsilon}
a high penalty term. Minimizing this objective results in a lower bound on
\epsilon
that renders (
189
) feasible. Therefore, it might give less conservative constraint tightening compared to the case where (
189
) is optimized with a user-chosen value for
\epsilon
.

Remark 21
.

Note that (
189
) is bilinear in
{X^{\delta}}
and
L
. Hence, a natural solution is to iterate between solving
{X^{\delta}}
and
L
. For a given matrix
{P^{\delta}}={X^{\delta}}^{-1}
, we use the following SDP to optimize
L
:

\displaystyle\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta^{2},\epsilon^{2},l^{2},\\
{\bar{H}},{\bar{H}_{1}}\end{subarray}}{{\operatorname{min}}}\ \

\displaystyle{\lambda^{\delta,\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}+{\lambda^{\delta}}\delta^{2}+c^{l}l^{2},

(196a)

\displaystyle\operatorname{s.t.}\

\displaystyle\mathrm{\text{\eqref{eq:rompc_sdp_lmi_rpi_delta}}},

(196b)

\displaystyle\mathrm{\text{\eqref{eq:rompc_sdp_lmi_rpi_eps}}},

(196c)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{P^{\delta}}&{P^{\delta}}LC\\
\left({P^{\delta}}LC\right)^{\top}&l^{2}{P^{\delta}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0,

(196d)

\displaystyle{\bm{\zeta}}\in{\mathcal{Z}},\quad{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\operatorname{vert}}\left({\mathcal{W}}\right),\quad{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H),

with additional decision variable
l
such that

\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\leq l,

(197)

enforced by LMI (
196d
) as proven in Appendix
I
, and corresponding penalty term
c^{l}
. By minimizing
l
, we minimize the upper bound on
\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}LC{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert
, thereby also reducing conservatism in the computation of
{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}
given in (
187
).

In conclusion, by successfully solving (
189
), we can find matrices
{P^{\delta}}
and
{K^{\delta}}({\bm{x}})
such that incremental Lyapunov function
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
is given by (
177
) and satisfies (
151a
) and (
152
). Furthermore, properties (
151b
), (
151c
), and (
151d
) are also satisfied by the design of contraction LMI (
189c
), RPI LMIs (
189d
) and (
189e
), and Lipschitz LMIs (
189f
), and (
189g
). Therefore, we can conclude that this design satisfies Assumption
7
. Furthermore, Assumption
8
is directly satisfied by the design of
\epsilon
-RPI LMI (
185
).

Terminal ingredients design

Similar to the terminal ingredients design presented in Section
4.3.4
, we need to design a suitable terminal cost
{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}},{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})
such that Assumption
9
is satisfied. In this case, we define the terminal control law as

{\kappa^{\mathrm{f}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{r}})}={\bm{u}^{\mathrm{r}}}+{\kappa^{\delta}}({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})={\bm{u}^{\mathrm{r}}}+\int_{0}^{1}{K^{\delta}({{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{x}}(s)})}ds\ ({\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}}).

(198)

Furthermore, matrix
P
needs to be defined such that descent bound (
162
) is satisfied:

\frac{d}{dt}\left(({\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{z}})^{\top}P({\hat{\bm{x}}}-{\bm{z}})\right)\leq-{{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{s}}}({\bm{z}},{\bm{v}},{\bm{r}}),

(199)

in which
{\dot{\hat{\bm{x}}}}\coloneqq f({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})})+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+L({\bm{y}}-C{\hat{\bm{x}}})
and
{\dot{\bm{z}}}=f({{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}
. Again, we find
P
with the lowest eigenvalues by solving SDP (
145
).

Thus, we can conclude that Assumption
9
is satisfied.

Reference trajectory design

Similar to the reference trajectory design in Section
4.3.4
, Assumptions
10
and
11
are trivially satisfied if the system and obstacle avoidance constraints used for generating the reference trajectory are constructed according to (
169
) and (
170
) using tightening constants
{c_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}},j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}}
and
{c^{\mathrm{o}}}
from SDP (
189
), respectively, and
\alpha
satisfying the lower bound computed in (
250
). Correspondingly, terminal set (
166
) is suitably designed around the reference trajectory to ensure closed-loop system and obstacle avoidance constraints satisfaction at all times.

5.4
Concluding remarks

In conclusion, the presented ROMPC scheme is an effective tool to provide safety guarantees for tracking dynamically feasible reference trajectories with a mobile robot described by disturbed dynamics (
39
) and noisy output measurements (
146
). The scheme is based on the idea that both the impact of disturbances and measurement noise can be upper-bounded by the combination of an
s
-sublevel set of incremental Lyapunov function
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
and an
\epsilon
-sublevel set of incremental Lyapunov function
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
. This upper bound starts from
\epsilon
to account for the observer error and grows using the dynamics of
s
, which results in a larger tube size than the one constructed in Section
4
. If we use this increased tube size to tighten the system and obstacle avoidance constraints imposed on the trajectory optimization starting from estimated state
{\hat{\bm{x}}}
, we can guarantee that the closed-loop system
({{\bm{x}},{\bm{u}}})
always satisfies the actual system and obstacle avoidance constraints. In this work, both Lyapunov functions use the same metric. In general, one could consider using different metrics to quantify the impact of disturbances and measurement noise
[
21
]
. Recursive feasibility is proven by constructing a minimum positive invariant terminal set based on
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
and
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
. This expression is similar to the one found in Section
4.3.2
with the inclusion of the impact of measurement noise. Furthermore, similar to Section
4.3.3
, trajectory tracking is proven by showing that terminal cost matrix
P
can be computed using the weighting matrix
{P^{\delta}}
used in
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
and
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
. The upper bound on the tracking error is less strict than the one derived in Section
4.3.3
, which is caused by the impact of measurement noise
{\bm{\eta}}
.

Appendix A
Proof of terminal set (
31
) invariance

In words, (
31
) tries to find the largest value for
\alpha
such that system constraints (
2
) are satisfied for all states satisfying terminal set constraint (
14
). Let’s define the vector pointing from reference state
{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}}
to state
{\bm{x}}
by

{\bm{\delta}}\coloneqq{\bm{x}}-{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}}.

(200)

Terminal set constraint (
14
) can now be rewritten as

\lVert{\bm{\delta}}\rVert_{P({\bm{r}})}^{2}={\bm{\delta}}^{\top}P({\bm{r}}){\bm{\delta}}\leq\alpha^{2}.

(201)

Furthermore, let’s define the error in the control input by

{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}^{\mathrm{r}}}\overset{\eqref{eq:tmpc_kappaf}}{=}K({\bm{r}})({\bm{x}}-{\bm{x}^{\mathrm{r}}})=K({\bm{r}}){\bm{\delta}}.

(202)

Thus, in the terminal set, it should hold that

{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\leq{l_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}-{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}{\bm{r}},\ j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}}.

(203)

Since the terminal set is described by (
201
), we would like the following implication to hold to satisfy (
15b
) in Assumption
1
:

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}P({\bm{r}}){\bm{\delta}}\leq\alpha^{2}\ \Rightarrow\ {L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\leq{l_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}-{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}{\bm{r}},\ \forall{\bm{r}}\in{\bar{\mathcal{Z}}},\ j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}}.

(204)

By defining
{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\coloneqq P({\bm{r}})^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\delta}}
we obtain

\displaystyle{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}^{\top}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\leq\alpha^{2}\ \Rightarrow\ {L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\leq{l_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}-{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}{\bm{r}},\ \forall{\bm{r}}\in{\bar{\mathcal{Z}}},\ j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}}.

(205)

Squaring and taking the norm of the left side of the implication gives

{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}^{\top}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\leq\alpha^{2}\ \Rightarrow\ \left\lVert{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left\lVert{l_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}-{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}{\bm{r}}\right\rVert^{2},\ \forall{\bm{r}}\in{\bar{\mathcal{Z}}},\ j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,{n^{\mathrm{s}}}]}}.

(206)

We can now write

\displaystyle\left\lVert{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\left\lVert{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert^{2}

(207)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{=}

\displaystyle\left\lVert{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert^{2}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}^{\top}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}

(208)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:xf_bdelta}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\left\lVert{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert^{2}\alpha^{2}

(209)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(c)}}{=}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left({L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}\alpha^{2}

(210)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(d)}}{=}

\displaystyle\left\lVert P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[K({\bm{r}})^{\top}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}^{\top}\right]{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}\alpha^{2},

(211)

where (a) follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, (b) follows from the definition of the inner product, (c) follows from the fact that the norm of a matrix is equal to the norm of its transpose, and (d) follows from the definition of the transpose and the fact that
P({\bm{r}})
is symmetric.
Thus, the following implication holds:

\left\lVert P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[K({\bm{r}})^{\top}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}^{\top}\right]{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}\alpha^{2}\leq\left({l_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}-{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}{\bm{r}}\right)^{2}\Rightarrow\left\lVert{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left({l_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}-{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}{\bm{r}}\right)^{2}.

(212)

Taking the square root of both sides gives

\left\lVert P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[K({\bm{r}})^{\top}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}^{\top}\right]{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}^{\top}\right\rVert\alpha\leq\left({l_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}-{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}{\bm{r}}\right)\Rightarrow\left\lVert{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}\begin{bmatrix}K({\bm{r}})\\
{I^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}}\end{bmatrix}P({\bm{r}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert\leq\left({l_{j}^{\mathrm{s}}}-{L_{j}^{\mathrm{r,s}}}{\bm{r}}\right).

(213)

Therefore, the maximum value for
\alpha
such that the system constraints are satisfied for all states satisfying terminal set constraint (
14
) is given by solving LP (
31
).∎

Appendix B
Proof of tube size upper bound (
68
)

Intuitively, (
68
) says that the upper bound on the candidate tube size starts
s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}
lower than
s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}
and contracts exponentially towards
s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}
with factor
e^{-\rho\tau}
from there. Thus, the upper bound on the candidate tube size contracts with factor
\rho
to the previously optimal tube size. This makes sense since the tube dynamics (
42e
) saturate exponentially to a maximum value of
\frac{{\bar{w}}}{\rho}
.

Let’s prove this by induction. At
\tau=0
, (
68
) is trivially satisfied. Given that (
68
) holds at
\tau=0
, we can prove that it also holds for
\tau>0
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\left(s_{\tau}-s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}+e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}\right)\overset{\eqref{eq:rmpc_sdot}}{=}

\displaystyle-\rho s_{\tau}+{\bar{w}}+\rho s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}-{\bar{w}}-\rho e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}

(214a)

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq:rmpc_sdot}}}{=}

\displaystyle-\rho\left(s_{\tau}-s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}+e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}\right)

(214b)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:s_bound}}{\geq}

\displaystyle 0.

(214c)

Since the value of
s_{\tau}-s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}+e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}
starts negative, its derivative is positive for
s_{\tau}-s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}+e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}<0
and zero for
s_{\tau}-s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}+e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}=0
,
s_{\tau}-s_{{T^{\mathrm{s}}}+\tau}+e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T^{\mathrm{s}}}
exponentially contracts to zero. Therefore, we can conclude that (
68
) holds for
\tau\geq 0
.∎

Appendix C
Proof of terminal set (
76
) invariance

To prove that terminal set (
76
) is invariant for
\tau\geq 0
, we need to show that if the terminal set constraint is satisfied at prediction time
T
at time
t
, i.e.,

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}}_{{T|t}}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T}}^{\mathrm{r}})}+s_{T}\leq\alpha,

(215)

the terminal set constraint should also be satisfied for
{T+\tau},\tau\geq 0
at time
t
, i.e.,

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T+\tau}}^{\mathrm{r}})}+s_{{T+\tau}}\leq\alpha.

(216)

Since there are no disturbances in this part of the prediction horizon, we know that terminal control law
{\kappa^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T+\tau}}^{\mathrm{r}})
ensures contraction of
\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T+\tau}}^{\mathrm{r}})}
by at least
\rho
as given by (
47
). Given terminal set satisfaction (
215
), this gives the following upper bound:

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T+\tau}}^{\mathrm{r}})}\leq e^{-\rho\tau}\left(\alpha-s_{T}\right).

(217)

If the terminal set is invariant, the right-hand side of this equation is upper-bounded by
\alpha-s_{{T+\tau}}
as derived from (
216
):

e^{-\rho\tau}\left(\alpha-s_{T}\right)\leq\alpha-s_{{T+\tau}},

(218)

which is trivially satisfied for
\tau=0
. Working out this inequality for
\tau>0
, similar to (
82
), gives

\displaystyle e^{-\rho\tau}\left(\alpha-s_{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha-s_{{T+\tau}}

(219a)

\displaystyle\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\alpha

\displaystyle\geq-e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T}+s_{{T+\tau}}

(219b)

\displaystyle\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\alpha

\displaystyle\geq-e^{-\rho\tau}\left(1-e^{-\rho T}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}}}{\rho}+\left(1-e^{-\rho({T+\tau})}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}}}{\rho}

(219c)

\displaystyle\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\left(-e^{-\rho\tau}+e^{-\rho({T+\tau})}+1-e^{-\rho({T+\tau})}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}}}{\rho}

(219d)

\displaystyle\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}}}{\rho}

(219e)

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\frac{{\bar{w}}}{\rho}.

(219f)

Thus, terminal set (
76
) is invariant provided that
\alpha\geq\frac{{\bar{w}}}{\rho}
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of RPI LMI (
138
)

The goal of this appendix is to design an LMI ensuring that sublevel set
{V^{\delta}({{\bm{x}},{\bm{z}}})}\leq\delta^{2}
is RPI for disturbed dynamics (
39
) and
{\bm{w}}={\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}\oplus{\mathcal{W}}
, with
{\mathcal{W}}
given by (
40
). Given state-independent
{P^{\delta}}
,
{V^{\delta}({{\bm{x}},{\bm{z}}})}
is given by (
133
). Consequently, we want the following property to hold:

{\frac{d}{dt}V^{\delta}({{\bm{x}},{\bm{z}}})}\leq 0,\quad\forall{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}\geq\delta^{2},\quad\forall{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\mathcal{W}},

(220)

with
{\bm{\delta}}
defined in (
48
).

Using the state-independency of
{P^{\delta}}
, we can remove the integral and simplify the upper bound on
{\frac{d}{dt}V^{\delta}({{\bm{x}},{\bm{z}}})}
in (
124b
) as follows:

{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}\left({A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}\right){\bm{\delta}}+{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}E{\bm{w}}^{0}+\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}\leq 0,

(221)

or, alternatively,

\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}{A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}&{P^{\delta}}E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}\leq 0.

(222)

We want this inequality to hold for all
{\bm{\delta}}
such that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}\geq\delta^{2}
and all
{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\mathcal{W}}
. Since
{\mathcal{W}}
is a polytopic set and
{\bm{w}}^{0}
appear linearly in the above inequality, it suffices to check the inequality for the vertices of
{\mathcal{W}}
, i.e., the inequality should hold for all
{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\operatorname{vert}}({\mathcal{W}})
.

To enforce these properties, we can use the S-procedure, which states that
\lambda F_{1}-F_{2}\succeq 0,\lambda\geq 0
is a sufficient condition for implication
{\bm{x}}^{\top}F_{1}{\bm{x}}\leq 0\implies{\bm{x}}^{\top}F_{2}{\bm{x}}\leq 0
to hold
[
48
]
. In this case, we leverage the S-procedure to enforce that the following implication holds:

{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}\geq\delta^{2}\implies\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}{A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}&{P^{\delta}}E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}\leq 0.

(223)

Re-ordering the terms on the left side of this implication gives

\delta^{2}-{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}\leq 0,

(224)

which can be written in matrix form as

\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}-{P^{\delta}}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}\leq 0.

(225)

Now, we can apply the S-procedure with

\displaystyle{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
1\end{bmatrix},

(226a)

\displaystyle F_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}-{P^{\delta}}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix},

(226b)

\displaystyle F_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}{A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}&{P^{\delta}}E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&0\end{bmatrix},

(226c)

to obtain

\lambda\begin{bmatrix}-{P^{\delta}}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}{A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}&{P^{\delta}}E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&0\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0.

(227)

Working out this LMI gives

\displaystyle-\begin{bmatrix}{A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}+\lambda{P^{\delta}}&{P^{\delta}}E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&-\lambda\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\succeq 0,

(228a)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}+\lambda{P^{\delta}}&{P^{\delta}}E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&-\lambda\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preceq 0,

(228b)

with
\lambda\geq 0
. Pre- and post-multiplying with
\begin{bmatrix}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&1\end{bmatrix}
yields

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}\left({A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}+\lambda{P^{\delta}}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-1}&{P^{\delta}}^{-1}\left({P^{\delta}}E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)\\
\left(\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-1}&-\lambda\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preceq 0,

(229a)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}{A^{\mathrm{cl}^{\top}}}+{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}+\lambda{P^{\delta}}^{-1}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}&-\lambda\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preceq 0.

(229b)

Filling in the definition of
{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}
gives

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{K^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\right)^{\top}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{K^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-1}+\lambda{P^{\delta}}^{-1}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}&-\lambda\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preceq 0,

(230a)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}{A({\bm{\zeta}})}^{\top}+{P^{\delta}}^{-1}{K^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}^{\top}{B({\bm{\zeta}})}^{\top}+{A({\bm{\zeta}})}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{K^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}+\lambda{P^{\delta}}^{-1}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}&-\lambda\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preceq 0,

(230b)

which, under the same coordinate transformation as in Section
4.3.4
with state-independent
{X^{\delta}}={P^{\delta}}^{-1}
and state-dependent
{Y^{\delta}}({\bm{x}})={K^{\delta}}({\bm{x}}){P^{\delta}}
, and leveraging the symmetry of
{P^{\delta}}
, gives the following LMI:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{X^{\delta}}{A({\bm{\zeta}})}^{\top}+{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}^{\top}{B({\bm{\zeta}})}^{\top}+{A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}+\lambda{X^{\delta}}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}&-\lambda\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preceq 0,

(231a)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}{X^{\delta}}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{Y^{\delta}({\bm{x}})}\right)^{\top}+\lambda{X^{\delta}}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}\right)^{\top}&-\lambda\delta^{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preceq 0.

(231b)

∎

Appendix E
Proof of LMI for Lipschitz continuity obstacle avoidance constraints (
139f
)

The goal of this appendix is to derive the LMI ensuring Lipschitz continuity of the obstacle avoidance constraints using tightening constant
{c^{\mathrm{o}}}
and incremental Lyapunov function
{V^{\delta}({{\bm{x}},{\bm{z}}})}
according to (
44c
). Following similar steps as in
[
32
]
, we first derive a Lipschitz bound on general constraints
{g_{j}}({{\bm{x}},{\bm{u}}})\leq 0
, i.e., a bound of the following form for
j\in{\mathbb{N}_{[1,n]}}
where
n
is the number of constraints:

{g_{j}}({{\bm{x}},{\bm{u}}})-{g_{j}}({\bm{z}},{\bm{v}})\leq{c_{j}}\lVert{\bm{x}}-{\bm{z}}\rVert_{P^{\delta}}.

(232)

The linearization of these constraints with respect to states
{\bm{x}}
and inputs
{\bm{u}}
is given by

\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}{\bm{\delta}},

(233)

with
{\bm{\delta}}
defined in (
48
). This gives the following Lipschitz bound:

\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}\leq{c_{j}}\lVert{\bm{\delta}}\rVert_{P^{\delta}},

(234)

in which we can rewrite the expression for
\lVert{\bm{\delta}}\rVert_{P^{\delta}}
as

\lVert{\bm{x}}-{\bm{z}}\rVert_{P^{\delta}}=\sqrt{{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}}=\sqrt{{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\delta}}}=\sqrt{\left({P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\delta}}\right)^{\top}\left({P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\delta}}\right)}=\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\delta}}\right\rVert,

(235)

to obtain

\left(\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}\right){\bm{\delta}}\leq{c_{j}}\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\delta}}\right\rVert.

(236)

Defining
{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\coloneqq{P^{\delta}}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\delta}}
gives the following inequality:

\left(\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\leq{c_{j}}\left\lVert{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert.

(237)

An upper bound on the left-hand side of this equation is given by

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\left(\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert

(238a)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\left(\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\left\lVert{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert.

(238b)

Thus, a sufficient condition for (
237
) to hold is

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\left\lVert{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq{c_{j}}\left\lVert{\tilde{{\bm{\delta}}}}\right\rVert

(239a)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g_{j}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}\right){P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq{c_{j}}.

(239b)

In the following, we derive the LMI that should hold for the obstacle avoidance constraints specifically. first note that obstacle avoidance constraints (
3
) are already linear and do not depend on inputs
{\bm{u}}
. Therefore, we can write the following equality:

\frac{\partial{g^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}+\frac{\partial{g^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\partial{\bm{u}}}\frac{\partial{\bm{u}}}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\rvert_{\bm{x}}={L^{\mathrm{o}}}M.

(240)

Filling this expression in in (
239
) gives the following inequality:

\left\lVert{L^{\mathrm{o}}}M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\leq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}.

(241)

As commented under (
3
), the polytopic obstacle avoidance constraints can always be normalized such that
\left\lVert{L^{\mathrm{o}}}\right\rVert=1
. Therefore, we can write

\left\lVert{L^{\mathrm{o}}}M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert{L^{\mathrm{o}}}\right\rVert\left\lVert M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert=\left\lVert M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert.

(242)

Thus, the following implication holds:

\left\lVert M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\leq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}\implies\left\lVert{L^{\mathrm{o}}}M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert\leq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}.

(243)

Working out the left-hand side of this implication and applying coordinate change
{X^{\delta}}\coloneqq{P^{\delta}}^{-1}
yields

\displaystyle\left\lVert M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\preceq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}

(244a)

\displaystyle\left\lVert M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\preceq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}

(244b)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\preceq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}

(244c)

\displaystyle\left\lVert{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}M^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\preceq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}

(244d)

\displaystyle\left({P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}M^{\top}\right)^{\top}\left({P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}M\right)

\displaystyle\preceq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}

(244e)

\displaystyle M{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}{P^{\delta}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}M^{\top}

\displaystyle\preceq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}

(244f)

\displaystyle M{P^{\delta}}^{-1}M^{\top}

\displaystyle\preceq{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}

(244g)

\displaystyle{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}-M{P^{\delta}}^{-1}M^{\top}

\displaystyle\succeq 0

(244h)

\displaystyle{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}-M{P^{\delta}}^{-1}{P^{\delta}}{P^{\delta}}^{-1}M^{\top}

\displaystyle\succeq 0

(244i)

\displaystyle{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}-M{X^{\delta}}{X^{\delta}}^{-1}{X^{\delta}}M^{\top}

\displaystyle\succeq 0.

(244j)

Taking the Schur complement of this LMI gives

\begin{bmatrix}{c^{\mathrm{o}}}^{2}{I^{{n^{\mathrm{p}}}}}&M{X^{\delta}}\\
\left(M{X^{\delta}}\right)^{\top}&{X^{\delta}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0.

(245)

∎

Appendix F
Proof of terminal set (
166
) invariance

The proof in this appendix follows similar steps as the one in Appendix
C
. To prove that terminal set (
166
) is invariant for
\tau\geq 0
, we need to show that if the terminal set constraint is satisfied at prediction time
T
at time
t
, i.e.,

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}}_{{T|t}}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T}}^{\mathrm{r}})}+s_{T}+\epsilon\leq\alpha,

(246)

the terminal set constraint should also be satisfied for
{T+\tau},\tau\geq 0
at time
t
, i.e.,

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T+\tau}}^{\mathrm{r}})}+s_{{T+\tau}}+\epsilon\leq\alpha.

(247)

Since there are no disturbances in this part of the prediction horizon, we know that terminal control law
{\kappa^{\mathrm{f}}}({\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T+\tau}}^{\mathrm{r}})
ensures contraction of
\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T+\tau}}^{\mathrm{r}})}
by at least
\rho
as given by (
47
). Given terminal set satisfaction (
246
), this gives the following upper bound:

\sqrt{{V^{\delta}}({\bm{z}}_{{T+\tau}|t}^{*},{\bm{x}}_{{t+T+\tau}}^{\mathrm{r}})}\leq e^{-\rho\tau}\left(\alpha-s_{T}-\epsilon\right).

(248)

If the terminal set is invariant, the right-hand side of this equation is upper-bounded by
\alpha-s_{{T+\tau}}-\epsilon
as derived from (
247
):

e^{-\rho\tau}\left(\alpha-s_{T}-\epsilon\right)\leq\alpha-s_{{T+\tau}}-\epsilon,

(249)

which is trivially satisfied for
\tau=0
. Working out this inequality for
\tau>0
, similar to (
219
), gives

\displaystyle e^{-\rho\tau}\left(\alpha-s_{T}-\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha-s_{{T+\tau}}-\epsilon

(250a)

\displaystyle\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\alpha

\displaystyle\geq-e^{-\rho\tau}s_{T}+s_{{T+\tau}}+\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\epsilon

(250b)

\displaystyle\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\alpha

\displaystyle\geq-e^{-\rho\tau}\left(1-e^{-\rho T}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+\left(1-e^{-\rho({T+\tau})}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\epsilon

(250c)

\displaystyle\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}+e^{-\rho({T+\tau})}+e^{-\rho({T+\tau})}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\epsilon

(250d)

\displaystyle\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+\left(1-e^{-\rho\tau}\right)\epsilon

(250e)

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+\epsilon.

(250f)

Thus, terminal set (
166
) is invariant provided that
\alpha\geq\frac{{\bar{w}^{\mathrm{o}}}}{\rho}+\epsilon
.
∎

Appendix G
Proof of
\epsilon
-RPI LMI (
185
)

The goal of this appendix is to design an LMI ensuring that sublevel set
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}\leq\epsilon^{2}
is RPI for the error between estimated state dynamics
{\dot{\hat{\bm{x}}}}
, given by (
5.3.4
) with
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathbb{R}^{{n^{\mathrm{y}}}}}
and
{\bm{\eta}}\in H
defined in (
146
) and
H
defined in (
147
), and disturbed dynamics
{\dot{\bm{x}}}
, given by (
39
) with
{\bm{w}}={\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}\oplus{\mathcal{W}}
and
{\mathcal{W}}
defined in (
40
).

Given state-independent
{P^{\delta}}
,
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
is given by

{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}\coloneqq({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}})^{\top}{P^{\delta}}({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}).

(251)

For the
\epsilon
-sublevel set of
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
to be RPI, we want the following property to hold:

{\frac{d}{dt}V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}\leq 0,\quad\forall{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\epsilon}}\geq\epsilon^{2},\quad\forall{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\mathcal{W}}\quad\forall{\bm{\eta}}\in H,

(252)

with
{\bm{\epsilon}}
defined in (
159
). Using the following additional shorthand notations
{\dot{{\bm{\epsilon}}}}
and
{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}
:

\displaystyle{\dot{{\bm{\epsilon}}}}

\displaystyle={\dot{\bm{x}}}-{\dot{\hat{\bm{x}}}},

\displaystyle=f({\bm{x}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))+E{\bm{w}}-\left(f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C({\bm{x}}-{\hat{\bm{x}}}))\right)

\displaystyle=f({\bm{x}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))-f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))+E{\bm{w}}^{0}-L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C{\bm{\epsilon}}),

(253)

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\coloneqq{A({{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{x}}},{{\bm{\gamma}}^{\mathrm{u}}})},

(254)

we can upper-bound the time-derivative of
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
as

\displaystyle{\frac{d}{dt}V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\dot{{\bm{\epsilon}}}})^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\epsilon}}+{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\dot{{\bm{\epsilon}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\dot{{\bm{\epsilon}}}}

(255a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(f({\bm{x}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))-f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))+E{\bm{w}}^{0}-L(F{\bm{\eta}}+C{\bm{\epsilon}})\right)

(255b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(f({\bm{x}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))-f({\hat{\bm{x}}},\kappa({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}))\right)-2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}LC{\bm{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)

(255c)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}A{\bm{\epsilon}}-2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}LC{\bm{\epsilon}}+2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)

(255d)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(A-LC\right){\bm{\epsilon}}+2{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)

(255e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\left(A-LC\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}\left(A-LC\right)\right){\bm{\epsilon}}+{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\epsilon}},

(255f)

where (a) is obtained by knowing that an
s\in[0,1]
exists, such that this expression holds by the MVT.

Setting the desired upper bound from (
252
) gives

\displaystyle{\frac{d}{dt}V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}\leq

\displaystyle{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}\left(\left(A-LC\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}\left(A-LC\right)\right){\bm{\epsilon}}+{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)+\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0,

(256)

or, alternatively,

\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\epsilon}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}\left(A-LC\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}\left(A-LC\right)&{P^{\delta}}\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\epsilon}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}\leq 0.

(257)

It suffices to enforce this inequality for
{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\operatorname{vert}}({\mathcal{W}})
and
{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H)
since
{\mathcal{W}}
and
H
are convex polytopic sets.

Following the S-procedure similar to the derivation in Appendix
D
results in the following LMI:

\begin{bmatrix}\left(A-LC\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}\left(A-LC\right)+{\lambda^{\epsilon}}{P^{\delta}}&{P^{\delta}}\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&-{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}\end{bmatrix}\preceq 0,

(258)

with multiplier
{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\geq 0
and for
{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\operatorname{vert}}({\mathcal{W}})
and
{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H)
.

From here, following the same steps as presented in Appendix
D
results in the following
\epsilon
-RPI LMI:

\begin{bmatrix}{X^{\delta}}\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}-LC\right)^{\top}+\left({A({\bm{\zeta}})}-LC\right){X^{\delta}}+{\lambda^{\epsilon}}{X^{\delta}}&E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\\
\left(E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}&-{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\epsilon^{2}\end{bmatrix}\preceq 0,

(259)

with
{\lambda^{\epsilon}}\geq 0
and for
{\bm{w}}^{0}\in{\operatorname{vert}}({\mathcal{W}})
and
{\bm{\eta}}\in{\operatorname{vert}}(H)
.

Note that this LMI is similar to the RPI LMI derived in Appendix
D
, but with additional terms proportional to
L
in the top-left block and with
E{\bm{w}}^{0}-LF{\bm{\eta}}
instead of
E{\bm{w}}^{0}
in the top-right and bottom-left blocks.
∎

Appendix H
Proof of
\delta
-RPI LMI (
188
)

The goal of this appendix is to derive the LMI ensuring that sublevel set
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}\leq\delta^{2}
is RPI for the error between estimated state dynamics
{\dot{\hat{\bm{x}}}}
, given by (
5.3.4
) with
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathbb{R}^{{n^{\mathrm{y}}}}}
and
{\bm{\eta}}\in H
defined in (
146
) and
H
defined in (
147
), and observer error
{\bm{\epsilon}}
, defined in (
159
), satisfying
{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\epsilon}}\leq\epsilon^{2}
, as enforced by LMI (
185
).

For the
\delta
-sublevel set of
{V^{\delta}({\bm{x}},{\hat{\bm{x}}})}
to be RPI, we want the following property to hold:

{\frac{d}{dt}V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}\leq 0,\quad\forall{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}\geq\delta^{2},\quad\forall{\bm{\epsilon}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\epsilon}}\leq\epsilon^{2},\quad\forall{\bm{\eta}}\in H,

(260)

with
{\bm{\delta}}
and
{\bm{\epsilon}}
defined in (
158
) and (
159
), respectively. Using the following shorthand notation for
{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}
:

{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}\coloneqq{A({\bm{\zeta}})}+{B({\bm{\zeta}})}{K^{\delta}({\bm{x}})},

(261)

we can upper-bound the time-derivative of
{V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}
as

\displaystyle{\frac{d}{dt}V^{\delta}({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\dot{{\bm{\delta}}}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}+{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\dot{{\bm{\delta}}}}

(262a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\dot{{\bm{\delta}}}}

(262b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(f({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})})+E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}+LF{\bm{\eta}}+LC{\bm{\epsilon}}-f({\bm{z}},{\bm{v}})-E{\bm{w}^{\mathrm{b}}}\right)

(262c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}\left(f({\hat{\bm{x}}},{\kappa({{\hat{\bm{x}}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{v}}})})-f({\bm{z}},{\bm{v}})\right)+2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}(LF{\bm{\eta}}+LC{\bm{\epsilon}})

(262d)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}{\bm{\delta}}+2{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}(LF{\bm{\eta}}+LC{\bm{\epsilon}})

(262e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}+{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}{\bm{\delta}}+(LF{\bm{\eta}}+LC{\bm{\epsilon}})^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}+{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}(LF{\bm{\eta}}+LC{\bm{\epsilon}})

(262f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}\left({A^{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}\right){\bm{\delta}}+(LC{\bm{\epsilon}})^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}+{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}LC{\bm{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+(LF{\bm{\eta}})^{\top}{P^{\delta}}{\bm{\delta}}+{\bm{\delta}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}LF{\bm{\eta}},

(262g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
{\bm{\epsilon}}\\
1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\top}{P^{\delta}}+{P^{\delta}}{A^{\mathrm{cl}}}&{P^{\delta}}LC&{P^{\delta}}LF{\bm{\eta}}\\
(LC)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&0^{{n^{\mathrm{x}}}}&\bm{0}\\
\left(LF{\bm{\eta}}\right)^{\top}{P^{\delta}}&\bm{0}&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\delta}}\\
{\bm{\epsilon}}\\
